It's just making it really hard to communicate.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about how your thoughts jump from one topic to another? Do you feel that your thoughts are connected or do they feel disjointed or unrelated to you?\n\nPatient: Like I was just saying with the overnight shift and the paint colors... in my head, I guess it feels like they connect for a split second. Like one thing just triggers the next. But as soon as the words actually come out of my mouth, I realize they're completely unrelated. It's just disjointed. I don't mean to do it, but my brain just skips around, and that's why everyone at work ends up looking at me like they have no idea what I'm talking about.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced any unusual perceptions, like hearing voices or seeing things that others don't, or had beliefs that others might find strange?\n\nPatient: I haven't really heard voices or seen things like that. But... I guess I do have some thoughts that other people tell me are strange. \n\nLike at work, on the unit. I'm sure the supervisors changed my shifts on purpose, and I know they watch me through the unit cameras to see if I'm unsafe. People keep telling me it's just because of staffing shortages, but... I know that's not true. I just know they're watching me.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about your belief that the supervisors are watching you through the cameras? Do you feel they are doing this all the time or only at certain times?\n\nPatient: It feels like it's all the time, honestly. Whenever I'm on the floor. I know they changed my shifts specifically so they could keep an eye on me. People keep saying, \"Oh, it's just staffing shortages, we're short-handed,\" but... I know that's not it. I know they're watching me through the unit cameras to see if I'm unsafe. It's not just a feeling, I'm sure of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever doubt or question your belief that the supervisors are watching you through the cameras, or does it feel absolutely certain to you all the time?\n\nPatient: I don't doubt it at all. It feels absolutely certain to me. Like I said, I'm just sure the supervisors changed my shifts on purpose, and they're watching me through those cameras to see if I'm unsafe. I know people keep trying to tell me it's just staffing shortages, but... I know what's really going on. I'm certain of it.\n\nDoctor: Has there ever been a time when you’ve questioned whether the cameras are really being used to watch you, or have you always felt completely sure about it with no doubt at all?\n\nPatient: I've never questioned it. I've always been completely sure about it.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about why you think the supervisors are watching you — do you feel they are trying to harm you or prevent you from doing something in particular?\n\nPatient: I don't think they're trying to harm me, exactly. They're watching me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any evidence or experiences that made you reconsider your belief that the supervisors are watching you through the cameras, or has your belief stayed exactly the same regardless of what’s happened?\n\nPatient: No, my belief has stayed exactly the same. I'm just sure the supervisors changed my shifts and they watch me through the unit cameras to see if I'm unsafe. I can’t keep my words in a straight line anymore. \n\nLike, I'll start talking about an overnight shift, and then somehow I end up jumping to soccer, or paint colors, or patient charts. I know I'm not staying on track, because I can literally see everyone getting lost while I'm speaking. It just comes out all over the place.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are being inserted into your mind by someone else or taken out, or is it more that your own thoughts just race or jump around on their own?\n\nPatient: No, I don't feel like anyone is putting thoughts in my head or taking them out. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
It's happening pretty much all the time now. Like I was just saying... that's really what brought me in, because I just can’t keep my words in a straight line anymore, no matter who I'm talking to.
